## Semantic Chunks
### Section: Introduction
- **Level**: `intro`
- **Hash**: `44feeab0c7044399090eba59bbfc167da41689f10ddd708d2d214f27d71cbb2c`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-evidence-should-teachers-collect-when-they-suspect-ai-use` (Similarity: 0.9616)

```text
If you suspect that a student used ChatGPT or another AI tool, it can be difficult to know how to investigate without making the student feel that a decision has already been made. The best approach is to focus on verification, not accusation.

Instead of saying, "I know you used AI," gather evidence, review the student's writing process, compare the assignment with previous work, check sources, and ask the student to explain how the work was created.

This gives the student a meaningful opportunity to provide context while helping the teacher determine whether the available evidence is consistent with unauthorized AI use.

Checkmark Plagiarism supports this process with AI detection , essay writing playback , static AI detection, plagiarism detection , autograding , and integrations with Canvas and Google Classroom .
```

### Section: Start With Questions, Not Conclusions
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `0df67e4ef6dedf6fce5eade08b8e9fb4d41f6b791983de45ddbc7465dc6efb1c`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-are-the-signs-that-a-student-used-chatgpt` (Similarity: 0.9030)

```text
A teacher may notice several warning signs: a sudden style change, an AI detector flag, unusually advanced vocabulary, unfindable citations, or a large text paste in document history. These signs justify a closer review, but they do not prove misconduct.

The first step should never be: "You used ChatGPT."

A much stronger starting point is: "I have a few questions about how you completed this assignment. Can you walk me through your process?"
```

### Section: Why Avoid an Immediate Accusation?
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `052077fea549c89f61dfe3e676ddefa1e28d0d7b90f81a7231d39be8ca6184c5`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-do-i-talk-to-a-student-i-suspect-of-using-ai` (Similarity: 0.8191)

```text
There are many legitimate explanations for unusual student submissions: extensive tutoring, revision with a writing center, drafting in an external application, or permitted grammar assistance. An accusation made too early turns a fact-finding conversation into an adversarial argument.
```

### Section: 1. Identify Exactly What Raised Your Concern
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `f8ae47188bdf22204c05f503441540d613cf8bac896b2535efaad49a172e4dc7`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-should-i-do-if-i-suspect-a-student-used-ai` (Similarity: 0.8847)

```text
Avoid relying on vague impressions like "it sounds like AI." Identify observable, concrete issues:

Vocabulary or syntax dramatically divergent from past work 
 Specific paragraphs that appear far more sophisticated than surrounding sections 
 Omission of required classroom-specific instructions 
 Nonexistent or hallucinated citations 
 Unusual paste events in writing history 
 AI detection flags concentrated in specific passages
```

### Section: 2. Compare the Assignment With Previous Student Work
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `48d8d9d05747a1a18039e83327874ff742c9a138c6d3d3523540c9d266c8191c`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-i-tell-if-a-student-used-ai-to-write-an-assignment` (Similarity: 0.8692)

```text
Compare the submission with earlier work across vocabulary, sentence structure, grammar, tone, and depth of analysis. An improved paper should not be treated as suspicious simply because it is better than past drafts—the question is whether the difference is significant enough to justify asking how the work was produced.
```

### Section: 3. Review the Assignment Before Looking at the AI Score
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `08afca46b2c720f66f07d955e08c72c0ccc1f71d72e02a8cd08dd294c76d0ca7`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-should-i-do-if-i-suspect-a-student-used-ai` (Similarity: 0.8149)

```text
Read the essay yourself first: Does it answer the prompt? Does it engage with assigned readings? Are arguments supported by specific evidence? Are there strange factual errors? Independent reading prevents score bias from clouding your evaluation.
```

### Section: 4. Use AI Detection as a Signal
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `bf59a19ef4f767a6bfb6cf61857521aecc723643fa07a108eef901e6d47d578f`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-should-i-do-if-i-suspect-a-student-used-ai` (Similarity: 0.8847)

```text
Treat detection results as: "This gives me another reason to ask questions," not "This proves the student cheated." Read more about detector interpretation in our guide on what does an AI detection percentage actually mean?
```

### Section: 5. Review How the Assignment Was Written
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `dc5b12545a6ce240bd10f449bd320b174ba03ab75d27737f8d069ca5b6074873`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-teachers-detect-ai-without-using-an-ai-detector` (Similarity: 0.8055)

```text
Two essays may appear equally polished, but one shows multi-session gradual drafting while the other appears all at once. Process evidence creates concrete, non-accusatory questions: "Can you explain where this section was drafted?"
```

### Section: 6. Remember That a Large Paste Does Not Prove AI Use
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `eba9773a08a8bfda665fee8391020c0cb1c11095ba1e7cb6374380be5b239591`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-should-i-do-if-a-student-cant-explain-their-own-essay` (Similarity: 0.9314)

```text
A large paste indicates inserted text, but the source could be an earlier draft, research notes, quotations, or external word processors. Always ask: "Where did this text come from?" and allow the student to provide earlier versions.
```

### Section: 7. Verify Sources and Citations
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `d4472202b855bbdb911d1cd33e0f69b2a2118e48d2a3a2c1263f23f4c7251332`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-should-i-do-if-i-suspect-a-student-used-ai` (Similarity: 0.9071)

```text
Verify whether cited articles, authors, and quotes exist. If a citation cannot be located, ask neutrally: "Can you show me where you found this source?" This addresses evidence directly without accusing the student of fabricating sources with AI.
```

### Section: 8. Ask the Student to Explain the Assignment
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `003fd0a596f94c763185316f2114d97d69576666b06c504a9d43e7572c9f2a12`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-should-i-do-if-i-suspect-a-student-used-ai` (Similarity: 0.8515)

```text
Ask neutral comprehension questions:

What is your central thesis? 
 Why did you choose this specific example? 
 What does this paragraph mean in your own words? 
 What does this specialized term mean here? 
 Which section was most difficult to write?

Look at the overall pattern of familiarity rather than expecting flawless recall from a nervous student.
```

### Section: 9. Ask the Student to Walk Through Their Writing Process
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `c321b7863e8bed246bf2c4bbeaf8089430ab0c01a6c333e105ee904faa976e12`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-should-i-do-if-a-student-cant-explain-their-own-essay` (Similarity: 0.9095)

```text
Ask: "Can you walk me through how you completed this assignment from beginning to end?" Inquire about where they began, how they drafted, what tools were used, and what revisions took place.
```

### Section: 10. Ask About AI Directly, but Neutrally
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `b2c68be9c9994e9de0ae6e72b9d2edf7582e3e8f0d2052a4867c2ad7653c3737`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/should-i-ask-a-student-to-explain-their-writing-if-i-suspect-ai` (Similarity: 0.9172)

```text
When appropriate, ask directly: "Did you use ChatGPT or another AI tool while completing this assignment?" If they answer yes, follow up with: "How did you use it?"
```

### Section: 11. Do Not Treat Any AI Use as Automatic Misconduct
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `7c25bf3061c504ea873dabf6ba2fd08530a5ca569c2c06a5f8036f30fdb8778d`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/is-an-ai-detector-enough-evidence-for-academic-misconduct` (Similarity: 0.9024)

```text
Brainstorming, outlining, grammar checking, sentence rewriting, and full essay generation are fundamentally different uses. Academic misconduct depends on whether the student's specific actions violated course policy.
```

### Section: 12. Give the Student a Chance to Provide Evidence
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `e10119717a595da849a2a981b4351e0d5b627b7724d14b16e5cd79acccebfcc3`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-questions-should-i-ask-a-student-about-a-suspicious-assignment` (Similarity: 0.8385)

```text
Allow the student to share earlier drafts, research notes, version histories, or outlines. A fair review must be capable of concluding that an initial suspicion was unjustified.
```

### Section: 13. Look for Consistency Across the Evidence
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `26425a79f8127b700e7ade1fb608d2642769218b94bd26d6fdd6ae0fcd2d5d03`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-i-tell-if-a-student-actually-wrote-their-essay` (Similarity: 0.8087)

```text
Evidence Supports the Student

Writing matches past student style 
 History shows gradual multi-day drafting 
 Student produces research notes & drafts 
 Citations are fully verified 
 Student fluently explains all arguments

Multiple Concerns Remain

Style is dramatically different from past work 
 Writing playback shows instant wholesale paste 
 Several citations do not exist in databases 
 Student cannot explain key concepts 
 Explanation conflicts with document history
```

### Section: 14. Keep the Conversation Focused on the Assignment
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `cadc7fc7e06780535f5b46ec5ab4f038e5061abbf74060b32686b173df9ff174`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-should-i-do-if-a-student-cant-explain-their-own-essay` (Similarity: 0.8325)

```text
Avoid personal or accusatory statements such as "This is too good for you." Focus strictly on observable evidence: "This writing style is different from your earlier work. Can you tell me about your revision process?"
```

### Section: 15. Explain Why You Are Asking
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `1e4f8c6b052ee3b76801e8ad6cc5c70b3a69a22412b33e0e5f3044e09fdd755f`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-i-tell-if-a-student-used-ai-to-write-an-assignment` (Similarity: 0.7943)

```text
Reassure the student by saying: "I'm trying to understand how the assignment was created before making any decisions." This establishes transparency and encourages honest dialogue.
```

### Section: 16. Compare What Happened With the Assignment Policy
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `44321e0d2ee3bfac8ff006cdbd59af834f1b26ebc6337957426b6f9f1db73b98`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/should-i-ask-a-student-to-explain-their-writing-if-i-suspect-ai` (Similarity: 0.8413)

```text
Determine: 1) What AI assistance occurred? 2) Was it allowed? 3) Was disclosure required? 4) Did the student comply? Academic integrity is tied to published expectations.
```

### Section: AI Detection Is Not the Same as Plagiarism Detection
- **Level**: `h2`

### Section: A Practical Verification Process
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `0ec3082bb6c58704bd533632bd4219c23c5ae8f8b320c788524fc902d8f26571`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-i-verify-who-actually-wrote-an-assignment` (Similarity: 0.8888)

```text
A Practical 13-Step Verification Sequence:

1. Identify the specific observable concern (not just "sounds like AI"). 
 2. Read the assignment independently before fixating on detector scores. 
 3. Compare it with previous student work baselines. 
 4. Review AI detection as one signal among many. 
 5. Examine writing playback when available to check drafting progression. 
 6. Verify sources, author names, and direct quotes. 
 7. Ask the student to explain the thesis and key concepts. 
 8. Ask the student to describe the writing process step-by-step. 
 9. Ask neutrally about AI and other digital tools used. 
 10. Allow the student to provide drafts, notes, or version histories. 
 11. Look for consistency across all collected evidence. 
 12. Compare any AI use with the specific assignment policy. 
 13. Document the review and follow established school procedures.
```
